## [1138] Mononeuritis multiplex: vasculitic causes, nerve biopsy indications

**Cluster**: Neurology / Rheumatology | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Mononeuritis multiplex is an asymmetric, asynchronous painful sensorimotor polyneuropathy involving multiple non-contiguous individual peripheral nerves, most frequently caused by systemic necrotizing vasculitis, requiring urgent electrodiagnostic confirmation, targeted nerve biopsy, and prompt immunosuppression.

### Core Teaching Points
- Peripheral Nerve Society consensus guidelines state that mononeuritis multiplex presenting with rapid, painful stepwise nerve deficits represents a medical emergency caused by ischemic infarction of the vasa nervorum from systemic necrotizing vasculitis (e.g., GPA, MPA, EGPA, PAN, cryoglobulinemic vasculitis). (PMID 21044230)
- Combined superficial peroneal nerve and peroneus brevis muscle biopsy significantly increases diagnostic sensitivity for systemic and non-systemic vasculitic neuropathy to over 80% compared with isolated sensory nerve biopsy, while minimizing motor deficit. (PMID 19343058, PMID 25231524)
- Cochrane systematic review evidence demonstrates that aggressive first-line immunosuppression with high-dose corticosteroids (intravenous pulse methylprednisolone followed by oral prednisone) combined with cyclophosphamide or rituximab arrests axonal infarction and prevents permanent motor disability. (PMID 17943881)
- Nerve conduction studies demonstrate asymmetric, non-uniform axonal sensory-motor neuropathy with reduced compound muscle action potential (CMAP) and sensory nerve action potential (SNAP) amplitudes, distinguishing vasculitic neuropathy from demyelinating polyneuropathies like CIDP or MMN. (PMID 21044230, PMID 25231524)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Comparative long-term efficacy and relapse prevention between rituximab and intravenous cyclophosphamide in non-systemic vasculitic neuropathy.
- Prognostic significance of persistent neuropathic pain following successful histological immunosuppressive remission.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Classic electrodiagnostic pattern of vasculitic neuropathy: asymmetric, multifocal axonal sensorimotor neuropathy (reduced amplitudes with preserved conduction velocities).
- Optimal biopsy site: combined superficial peroneal sensory nerve and peroneus brevis muscle biopsy.
- Differential diagnosis including non-vasculitic causes: multifocal motor neuropathy (MMN), CIDP (Lewis-Sumner syndrome), diabetes (diabetic amyotrophy), and sarcoidosis.

### Case Generation Hooks
- A 56-year-old man presenting with acute right wrist drop followed 4 days later by severe burning left foot numbness and left foot drop.
- A patient with eosinophilic granulomatosis with polyangiitis (EGPA) who develops severe bilateral lower extremity asymmetric motor weakness during an asthma flare.

**Reviewer Gap**: Prospective trials evaluating high-resolution peripheral nerve ultrasound and MR neurography as non-invasive alternatives to surgical nerve biopsy.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 21044230**: *Consensus guidelines on the classification, diagnosis and management of peripheral nerve vasculitis* (2010) [guideline] - J Peripher Nerv Syst
- **PMID 19343058**: *Nonsystemic vasculitic neuropathy: insights into diagnosis and treatment* (2009) [review] - Nat Rev Neurol
- **PMID 17943881**: *Immunosuppressive treatment for peripheral nerve vasculitis* (2007) [meta-analysis] - Cochrane Database Syst Rev
- **PMID 25231524**: *Vasculitic neuropathy* (2014) [review] - Lancet Neurol

## [1139] morbilliform rash

**Cluster**: Dermatology / Clinical Pharmacology | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Morbilliform (maculopapular) exanthem is the most common cutaneous drug reaction, characterized by symmetric erythematous macules and papules developing 7 to 14 days after drug exposure, requiring rapid differentiation from life-threatening severe cutaneous adverse reactions such as DRESS, SJS/TEN, and AGEP.

### Core Teaching Points
- Exanthematous drug eruptions typically appear 7 to 14 days after starting a new medication (or within 1-2 days upon rechallenge), characteristically originating on the trunk and proximal extremities before spreading symmetrically and centrifugally with pruritus and low-grade or absent fever. (PMID 7935686, PMID 22738099)
- The RegiSCAR validation scoring system differentiates uncomplicated morbilliform drug rashes from Drug Reaction with Eosinophilia and Systemic Symptoms (DRESS) based on fever >=38.5 C, enlarged lymph nodes (>=2 sites), atypical lymphocytes, eosinophilia, and internal organ involvement (hepatic, renal, pulmonary, or cardiac). (PMID 23855313)
- Critical clinical red flags signaling a life-threatening severe cutaneous adverse reaction (SCAR) include mucosal involvement (erosions of oral, ocular, or genital mucosa), cutaneous pain or tenderness, epidermal detachment/blistering (positive Nikolsky sign), facial edema, and palpable purpura. (PMID 7935686, PMID 22738099)
- DRESS syndrome frequently involves late reactivation of human herpesviruses (HHV-6, HHV-7, EBV, CMV) and demonstrates prolonged, relapsing systemic inflammation that persists for weeks to months despite discontinuation of the culprit drug, requiring prolonged oral corticosteroid tapers. (PMID 17584323, PMID 23855313)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Safe reintroduction protocols for essential antibiotics (e.g., aminopenicillins) in patients with a history of mild non-immediate childhood exanthems.
- Steroid-sparing efficacy of cyclosporine or tofacitinib in refractory DRESS with severe myocarditis or hepatitis.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Timeline of onset: simple morbilliform drug eruption occurs 7-14 days post-exposure, whereas DRESS exhibits a delayed latency of 2-8 weeks.
- Red flags for SCAR: mucosal involvement, cutaneous pain, facial edema, and fever.
- High-risk culprit drugs for DRESS: allopurinol, aromatic antiepileptics (carbamazepine, phenytoin, lamotrigine), and sulfonamides.

### Case Generation Hooks
- A 24-year-old woman prescribed amoxicillin-clavulanate 9 days ago who presents with a diffuse, itchy maculopapular rash on trunk and limbs, normal mucosal membranes, and no fever.
- A 52-year-old man starting allopurinol 4 weeks ago presenting with facial edema, widespread erythema, temperature 39.0 C, ALT 380 U/L, and absolute eosinophils 2.4 x 10^9/L.

**Reviewer Gap**: Prospective validation of point-of-care pharmacogenomic testing (e.g., HLA-B*58:01, HLA-B*15:02) in universal electronic prescribing alerts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 7935686**: *Severe adverse cutaneous reactions to drugs* (1994) [review] - N Engl J Med
- **PMID 23855313**: *The RegiSCAR scoring system for identifying Drug Reaction with Eosinophilia and Systemic Symptoms (DRESS)* (2013) [trial] - Br J Dermatol
- **PMID 17584323**: *Drug reaction with eosinophilia and systemic symptoms (DRESS): diagnostic criteria, viral reactivation, and therapeutic approach* (2007) [review] - J Dermatol

## [1140] Moyamoya disease: clinical presentations, surgical revascularisation indications

**Cluster**: Neurosurgery / Neurology | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Moyamoya disease is a progressive, occlusive cerebrovascular arteriopathy of the terminal internal carotid arteries and circle of Willis accompanied by compensatory fragile basal collateral vessels ('puffs of smoke'), causing recurrent ischemic stroke in children and intracranial hemorrhage in adults.

### Core Teaching Points
- In the randomized multicenter JAM trial by Miyamoto and colleagues, extracranial-intracranial (EC-IC) direct arterial bypass surgery (superficial temporal artery to middle cerebral artery, STA-MCA) significantly reduced the incidence of recurrent hemorrhagic stroke from 31.6% in the non-surgical arm down to 11.9% (HR 0.35; p=0.042) in adult moyamoya disease. (PMID 24699060)
- Angiographic evaluation adheres to the 6 Suzuki stages, progressing from initial carotid fork stenosis (Stage 1), expansion of basal moyamoya collateral vessels (Stages 2-3), to progressive collateral regression and reliance on transdural external carotid collaterals (Stages 5-6). (PMID 5765034)
- Bimodal age distribution dictates distinct clinical presentations: pediatric patients (peak age ~5-10 years) present predominantly with ischemic transient ischemic attacks or infarctions triggered by hyperventilation (crying, blowing on hot food), whereas adults (peak age ~40-45 years) present with ischemic stroke or intraventricular/subarachnoid hemorrhage from rupture of fragile collateral microaneurysms. (PMID 18940695, PMID 19297575)
- Surgical revascularization strategies combine direct bypass (STA-MCA anastomosis, providing immediate augmentation of cerebral blood flow) and indirect bypass (encephalo-duro-arterio-synangiosis [EDAS] or pial synangiosis, which promotes neoangiogenesis over months, especially effective in pediatric populations). (PMID 19297575, PMID 18940695)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Identification and postoperative hemodynamic monitoring of cerebral hyperperfusion syndrome following direct STA-MCA bypass.
- Clinical utility of universal genetic screening for the RNF213 p.R4810K founder variant in East Asian populations.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- JAM trial primary finding: direct EC-IC bypass significantly reduces recurrent hemorrhage in hemorrhagic adult moyamoya disease.
- Classic physiological trigger for pediatric moyamoya TIAs: hyperventilation causing hypocapnia and cerebral vasoconstriction.
- Pathognomonic conventional catheter angiographic appearance: 'puff of smoke' collateral network at the skull base.

### Case Generation Hooks
- A 7-year-old child who develops transient right hemiparesis and aphasia while crying intensely or blowing vigorously on a hot bowl of soup.
- A 42-year-old woman presenting with sudden headache, intraventricular hemorrhage, and digital subtraction angiography demonstrating bilateral terminal ICA occlusion and basal collaterals.

**Reviewer Gap**: Prospective trials evaluating whether dual antiplatelet therapy reduces microembolic ischemic events without escalating catastrophic collateral hemorrhage risk.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 24699060**: *Study Design for a Randomized Controlled Trial of Extracranial-Intracranial Bypass Surgery for Moyamoya Disease: JAM Trial* (2014) [trial] - Stroke
- **PMID 5765034**: *Cerebrovascular 'moyamoya' disease: disease showing abnormal net-like vessels in base of brain* (1969) [trial] - Arch Neurol
- **PMID 18940695**: *Moyamoya disease: current concepts and future perspectives* (2008) [review] - Lancet Neurol
- **PMID 19297575**: *Moyamoya disease and moyamoya syndrome* (2009) [review] - N Engl J Med

## [1141] MPN Blast Phase Management

**Cluster**: Haematology / Oncology | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Progression of myeloproliferative neoplasms (polycythemia vera, essential thrombocythemia, myelofibrosis) to the blast phase (MPN-BP) is defined by >=20% blasts in peripheral blood or bone marrow; it represents a biologically aggressive acute myeloid leukemia with dismal prognosis, managed by hypomethylating agent combinations as a bridge to allogeneic hematopoietic cell transplantation.

### Core Teaching Points
- According to the MPN Research Consortium consensus statement, MPN blast phase (MPN-BP) is defined by >=20% blasts in bone marrow or blood and carries a median overall survival under 6 months with standard supportive care or conventional induction chemotherapy. (PMID 34464478)
- In multi-institutional cohort studies by Badar and colleagues, hypomethylating agents (azacitidine or decitabine) combined with the BCL-2 inhibitor venetoclax achieved superior overall response rates (40-50%) and improved median survival compared with intensive 7+3 anthracycline-cytarabine chemotherapy (which carries excessive induction mortality >20-30%). (PMID 32087561)
- Allogeneic hematopoietic cell transplantation (allo-HCT) remains the only curative therapy; patients who achieve an objective morphological blast clearance (<5% blasts) prior to transplant achieve durable 3-year post-transplant survival of 30-40%. (PMID 34464478, PMID 23365459)
- Passamonti and colleagues identified independent risk factors for leukemic transformation in primary myelofibrosis, including circulating blast count >=3%, platelet count <100 x 10^9/L, and high molecular risk (HMR) mutations (ASXL1, SRSF2, IDH1/2, EZH2). (PMID 32097444)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Continuation versus discontinuation of JAK2 inhibitors (ruxolitinib) when combining hypomethylating agents with venetoclax in MPN-BP.
- Conditioning regimen intensity (reduced-intensity vs myeloablative) for allo-HCT in older patients with transformed secondary AML.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Diagnostic blast threshold defining blast phase transformation in MPN: >=20% blasts in blood or marrow.
- Only established curative treatment modality for MPN blast phase: allogeneic stem cell transplantation.
- Poor outcomes and high induction mortality associated with standard 7+3 intensive cytarabine/daunorubicin in transformed MPN-BP.

### Case Generation Hooks
- A 68-year-old man with an 8-year history of JAK2-positive polycythemia vera presenting with rapid weight loss, increasing splenomegaly, and peripheral blood showing 24% myeloblasts.
- A fit 54-year-old woman with post-essential thrombocythemia secondary AML receiving azacitidine and venetoclax as a bridge toward HLA-matched sibling allogeneic donor transplant.

**Reviewer Gap**: Prospective randomized phase 3 trials comparing novel targeted epigenetic/targeted combinations against hypomethylating agent monotherapy in MPN-BP.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 34464478**: *Management of myeloproliferative neoplasm in blast phase: a consensus report from the MPN Research Consortium* (2021) [guideline] - Blood Adv
- **PMID 32087561**: *Contemporary outcomes of blast phase myeloproliferative neoplasms* (2020) [trial] - Cancer
- **PMID 23365459**: *Management of the blast phase of essential thrombocythemia and polycythemia vera* (2013) [review] - Blood
- **PMID 32097444**: *Life expectancy and blast progression in primary myelofibrosis: a study on 1,002 patients* (2020) [trial] - Blood

## [1142] Mpox (monkeypox): transmission, clinical features, tecovirimat, vaccination, public health response

**Cluster**: Infectious Diseases / Public Health | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Mpox (formerly monkeypox) is an orthopoxvirus zoonosis that caused a declared Public Health Emergency of International Concern in 2022-2023, transmitted predominantly through close physical and sexual contact, presenting with painful mucosal and cutaneous lesions, managed with supportive care, targeted tecovirimat, and MVA-BN vaccination.

### Core Teaching Points
- In an international collaborative study across 16 countries by Thornhill and colleagues, 98% of persons with mpox infection during the 2022-2023 outbreak were gay or bisexual men, with transmission occurring via close physical and sexual contact in 95%, presenting with anogenital mucosal lesions (73%), proctitis, and painful pharyngitis. (PMID 35867790)
- Diagnostic confirmation requires polymerase chain reaction (PCR) testing of swab specimens obtained directly from the base or exudate of cutaneous or mucosal lesions; serological testing lacks specificity due to orthopoxvirus cross-reactivity. (PMID 35867790, PMID 35941374)
- Tecovirimat (TPOXX), which inhibits the orthopoxvirus VP37 envelope wrapping protein, is indicated under expanded access protocols for severe disease (extensive mucosal involvement, severe proctitis, ocular disease, or immunosuppression, particularly advanced HIV with CD4 <200 cells/mm3). (PMID 36327156, PMID 35941374)
- The modified vaccinia Ankara-Bavarian Nordic (MVA-BN / JYNNEOS) replication-deficient vaccine demonstrated high real-world vaccine effectiveness, with CDC data showing a 14-fold higher incidence of infection in unvaccinated persons compared to those receiving the 2-dose subcutaneous series. (PMID 36201401)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Efficacy of oral tecovirimat in mild-to-moderate clade I versus clade II mpox in placebo-controlled randomized trials (PALM007 trial).
- Durability of protection beyond 2 years following two doses of MVA-BN vaccine in people living with HIV.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Primary diagnostic modality: PCR swab from the base of active skin/mucosal vesicles or ulcers.
- Mechanism of action of tecovirimat (TPOXX): inhibition of the viral VP37 protein preventing extracellular viral egress.
- Features of severe necrotizing mpox in patients with advanced HIV infection (CD4 <200 cells/mm3).

### Case Generation Hooks
- A 31-year-old man presenting with severe tenesmus, rectal bleeding, perianal umbilicated pustules, and painful inguinal lymphadenopathy.
- An immunocompromised patient with CD4 count 45 cells/mm3 developing confluent necrotizing cutaneous lesions across the face and limbs requiring tecovirimat.

**Reviewer Gap**: Randomized clinical trials establishing the clinical impact of tecovirimat on lesion healing time in non-severe outpatient disease.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 35867790**: *Monkeypox Virus Infection in Humans across 16 Countries - April-June 2022* (2022) [trial] - N Engl J Med
- **PMID 36327156**: *Tecovirimat (TPOXX) expanded access protocol and safety for mpox treatment* (2022) [guideline] - MMWR Morb Mortal Wkly Rep
- **PMID 36201401**: *Incidence of monkeypox among unvaccinated persons compared with persons receiving MVA-BN (JYNNEOS) vaccine* (2022) [trial] - MMWR Morb Mortal Wkly Rep
- **PMID 35941374**: *Monkeypox: a contemporary review for healthcare professionals* (2022) [review] - Nat Med

## [1143] MRI in inflammatory arthritis: bone marrow oedema, erosions, tenosynovitis scoring

**Cluster**: Rheumatology / Musculoskeletal Radiology | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) is a sensitive imaging modality in inflammatory arthritis, detecting subclinical synovitis, tenosynovitis, and bone marrow edema (osteitis) months before structural erosions appear on conventional plain radiography.

### Core Teaching Points
- The Rheumatoid Arthritis MRI Score (RAMRIS) system validated by OMERACT standardizes evaluation of the wrist and metacarpophalangeal joints, semi-quantitatively grading bone marrow edema (osteitis), synovitis, and bone erosion. (PMID 12784422, PMID 28249826)
- Bone marrow edema (osteitis) on fat-suppressed T2-weighted or short tau inversion recovery (STIR) sequences represents true histological intra-osseous inflammatory cellular infiltration and is the single strongest imaging predictor of subsequent bone erosion at that exact anatomical site. (PMID 28249826, PMID 17133543)
- In rheumatoid arthritis patients in clinical remission according to conventional disease activity scores (DAS28), high-resolution MRI reveals persistent subclinical synovitis and tenosynovitis in over 70% of patients, predicting disease flare and progressive structural damage. (PMID 17133543)
- In axial spondyloarthritis, the SPARCC scoring system quantifies bone marrow edema reflecting acute sacroiliitis, where active subchondral inflammation is required to meet ASAS criteria for non-radiographic axial spondyloarthritis (nr-axSpA). (PMID 16142890)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Whether escalating DMARD therapy based purely on subclinical MRI inflammation in patients in clinical remission improves 5-year radiographic and functional outcomes.
- Distinguishing physiological mechanical bone marrow edema in athletes from pathological axial spondyloarthritis sacroiliitis.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Strongest MRI predictor of future irreversible bone erosion: bone marrow edema (osteitis) on STIR/fat-suppressed T2 imaging.
- Three core components of the OMERACT RAMRIS system: synovitis, bone marrow edema, and bone erosion.
- Definition of active sacroiliitis on MRI according to ASAS: subchondral bone marrow edema on STIR sequences in typical locations.

### Case Generation Hooks
- A 32-year-old woman with inflammatory hand pain, normal plain radiographs, and positive anti-CCP antibodies whose hand MRI demonstrates extensive flexor tenosynovitis and carpal osteitis.
- A 26-year-old man with chronic inflammatory back pain and normal pelvic X-rays whose sacroiliac joint MRI reveals prominent bilateral juxta-articular bone marrow edema.

**Reviewer Gap**: Cost-effectiveness and real-world clinical algorithms defining when extremity MRI alters management compared with musculoskeletal ultrasound.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 12784422**: *An introduction to the EULAR-OMERACT rheumatoid arthritis MRI reference image library and the RAMRIS scoring system* (2003) [guideline] - J Rheumatol
- **PMID 28249826**: *The OMERACT MRI inflammatory arthritis scoring system: validation of bone marrow edema (osteitis), synovitis, and bone erosion measurement* (2017) [guideline] - Ann Rheum Dis
- **PMID 17133543**: *Presence of significant synovitis in rheumatoid arthritis patients in clinical remission: an MRI study* (2006) [trial] - Arthritis Rheum
- **PMID 16142890**: *The Spondyloarthritis Research Consortium of Canada (SPARCC) magnetic resonance imaging index for assessment of sacroiliitis and spinal inflammation* (2005) [guideline] - J Rheumatol

## [1144] MRSA bacteremia treatment

**Cluster**: Infectious Diseases / Critical Care | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Methicillin-resistant Staphylococcus aureus (MRSA) bacteremia carries high 30-day mortality (>20%) and metastatic seeding risk; management requires prompt focus control, echocardiographic screening, and targeted bactericidal antimicrobial therapy with vancomycin (monitored by AUC/MIC) or daptomycin.

### Core Teaching Points
- In the randomized CAMERA2 trial, adding an antistaphylococcal beta-lactam (flucloxacillin or cefazolin) to standard therapy with vancomycin or daptomycin in MRSA bacteremia did not improve 90-day mortality (35% vs 32%; p=0.42) and was terminated early due to a significant excess in acute kidney injury (23% vs 6%; p<0.001). (PMID 32044943)
- IDSA and consensus guidelines recommend target vancomycin 24-hour area under the curve to minimum inhibitory concentration (AUC24/MIC) ratios of 400 to 600 mg*h/L using Bayesian dosing software, replacing traditional trough-only monitoring (15-20 mcg/mL) to reduce nephrotoxicity while preserving bactericidal efficacy. (PMID 32191793, PMID 21208910)
- In a randomized trial by Fowler and colleagues, high-dose daptomycin (6-10 mg/kg IV once daily) was non-inferior to standard therapy (vancomycin) for S. aureus bacteremia and right-sided endocarditis, serving as the primary bactericidal alternative for isolates with vancomycin MIC >1 mcg/mL. (PMID 16914701)
- Mandatory clinical management standards in MRSA bacteremia include repeat blood cultures at 48 to 72 hours until clearance is documented, echocardiography (transesophageal echocardiography preferred) to rule out infective endocarditis, and urgent source control (removal of infected intravascular catheters and surgical drainage of metastatic abscesses). (PMID 21208910)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Efficacy of daptomycin plus ceftaroline combination therapy in persistent refractory MRSA bacteremia with high vancomycin MIC.
- Optimal treatment duration (2 weeks vs 4-6 weeks) in low-risk uncomplicated catheter-associated MRSA bacteremia.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Findings of the CAMERA2 trial: beta-lactam addition to vancomycin did not improve mortality and caused high rates of acute renal failure.
- Contemporary monitoring target for vancomycin: AUC24/MIC ratio 400-600 rather than empirical high trough levels.
- Contraindication to daptomycin: pulmonary infections (e.g., MRSA pneumonia) due to inactivation by pulmonary surfactant.

### Case Generation Hooks
- A 58-year-old hemodialysis patient with persistent MRSA blood cultures 5 days after tunneled line removal despite vancomycin therapy (MIC 2.0 mcg/mL).
- A patient receiving vancomycin whose trough is 24 mcg/mL and serum creatinine rises from 85 to 195 micromol/L transitioning to AUC-guided dosing.

**Reviewer Gap**: Prospective trials evaluating oral step-down therapy with linezolid or tedizolid after initial intravenous clearance in uncomplicated MRSA bacteremia.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 32044943**: *Effect of Vancomycin or Daptomycin With vs Without an Antistaphylococcal beta-Lactam on Mortality, Bacteremia, Relapse, or Treatment Failure in Patients With MRSA Bacteremia (CAMERA2)* (2020) [trial] - JAMA
- **PMID 32191793**: *Therapeutic monitoring of vancomycin for serious methicillin-resistant Staphylococcus aureus infections: A revised consensus guideline* (2020) [guideline] - Am J Health Syst Pharm
- **PMID 16914701**: *Daptomycin versus standard therapy for bacteremia and endocarditis caused by Staphylococcus aureus* (2006) [trial] - N Engl J Med
- **PMID 21208910**: *Clinical practice guidelines by the Infectious Diseases Society of America for the treatment of methicillin-resistant Staphylococcus aureus infections in adults and children* (2011) [guideline] - Clin Infect Dis

## [1145] MRSA: decolonisation, mupirocin, chlorhexidine, glycopeptides, daptomycin, linezolid

**Cluster**: Infectious Diseases / Infection Control | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Hospital-associated MRSA transmission and invasive disease are mitigated by evidence-based infection control decolonization bundles and targeted systemic antimicrobial therapeutics, including glycopeptides, lipopeptides, and oxazolidinones tailored to site-specific pharmacokinetics.

### Core Teaching Points
- In the cluster-randomized REDUCE MRSA trial by Huang and colleagues evaluating 74,256 ICU patients, universal decolonization (daily chlorhexidine bathing plus twice-daily nasal mupirocin for all ICU patients) was significantly superior to targeted decolonization, reducing MRSA clinical isolates by 37% and all-cause bloodstream infections by 44%. (PMID 23718156)
- In non-ICU patients with medical devices, the cluster-randomized ABATE Infection trial demonstrated that daily chlorhexidine bathing and mupirocin decolonization significantly reduced clinical multidrug-resistant cultures and all-cause bloodstream infections by over 30%. (PMID 30850112)
- In hospital-acquired MRSA pneumonia, randomized trial evidence by Wunderink and colleagues showed that linezolid achieved higher clinical cure rates than vancomycin (57.6% vs 46.6%; p=0.042) due to superior penetration into pulmonary epithelial lining fluid. (PMID 22247123)
- Infectious Diseases Society of America (IDSA) guidelines emphasize that daptomycin is strictly contraindicated in MRSA pneumonia because it is bound and inactivated by pulmonary surfactant, but is a first-line bactericidal agent for MRSA skin and soft-tissue infections and bacteremia. (PMID 21208910)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Development of mupirocin resistance (mupA gene) during widespread universal intensive care decolonization protocols.
- Comparative long-term safety and hematologic toxicity of linezolid vs tedizolid in courses exceeding 14 days.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Key finding of REDUCE MRSA trial: universal decolonization outclasses targeted screening and contact precautions in reducing ICU bloodstream infections.
- Inactivation of daptomycin by alveolar surfactant preventing its use in pneumonia.
- Hematologic and neurological toxicities of linezolid (myelosuppression, optic and peripheral neuropathy, serotonin syndrome with SSRIs).
- Mupirocin nasal ointment mechanism: inhibition of bacterial isoleucyl-tRNA synthetase.

### Case Generation Hooks
- An ICU director evaluating evidence between routine universal daily 2% chlorhexidine bathing vs PCR admission surveillance for MRSA.
- A hospitalized patient receiving daptomycin for cellulitis who develops hospital-acquired MRSA pneumonia requiring immediate antimicrobial change.

**Reviewer Gap**: Surveillance studies evaluating high-level chlorhexidine (qacA/B genes) and mupirocin resistance patterns under decade-long hospital-wide universal bathing protocols.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 23718156**: *Targeted versus universal decolonization to prevent ICU infection (REDUCE MRSA)* (2013) [trial] - N Engl J Med
- **PMID 30850112**: *Chlorhexidine versus routine bathing to prevent multidrug-resistant organism infection and clinical cultures in non-ICU patients (ABATE Infection)* (2019) [trial] - Lancet
- **PMID 22247123**: *Linezolid in methicillin-resistant Staphylococcus aureus hospital-acquired pneumonia* (2012) [trial] - Clin Infect Dis
- **PMID 21208910**: *Clinical practice guidelines by the Infectious Diseases Society of America for the treatment of methicillin-resistant Staphylococcus aureus infections in adults and children* (2011) [guideline] - Clin Infect Dis

## [1146] MSI-high metastatic colorectal cancer immunotherapy

**Cluster**: Medical Oncology / Gastroenterology | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Microsatellite instability-high (MSI-H) or mismatch repair-deficient (dMMR) metastatic colorectal cancer represents an immunogenic tumor subtype characterized by dense neoantigen load, which responds dramatically to first-line immune checkpoint inhibition with pembrolizumab or nivolumab plus ipilimumab.

### Core Teaching Points
- In the landmark phase 3 KEYNOTE-177 trial, first-line pembrolizumab monotherapy significantly doubled progression-free survival compared with standard chemotherapy (FOLFOX/FOLFIRI +/- bevacizumab or cetuximab) (median PFS 16.5 vs 8.2 months; HR 0.60; p=0.0002) in MSI-H/dMMR metastatic colorectal cancer. (PMID 33264544)
- Long-term 5-year updates of KEYNOTE-177 confirmed sustained durability: 5-year PFS was 34.0% with pembrolizumab versus 7.6% with chemotherapy, with significantly lower grade 3-5 treatment-related adverse events (22% vs 66%). (PMID 35427471)
- In the CheckMate 142 trial, dual immune checkpoint blockade with nivolumab plus low-dose ipilimumab as first-line therapy achieved an objective response rate of 69%, a disease control rate of 84%, and a 24-month progression-free survival rate of 74% in dMMR/MSI-H mCRC. (PMID 29355075, PMID 34637336)
- Mismatch repair deficiency causes a thousand-fold increase in replication error mutations, generating abundant frameshift-derived neoepitopes that stimulate dense tumor-infiltrating lymphocytes, conferring susceptibility to PD-1/CTLA-4 axis blockade. (PMID 26028255, PMID 36307056)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Direct comparison of single-agent anti-PD-1 (pembrolizumab) versus dual checkpoint inhibition (nivolumab plus ipilimumab) in first-line MSI-H mCRC.
- Optimal management of the ~30% of patients who experience primary resistance and early progressive disease within the first 4 months of pembrolizumab.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- First-line standard of care for MSI-H/dMMR metastatic colorectal cancer: pembrolizumab monotherapy (KEYNOTE-177).
- Mechanistic basis for immunotherapy sensitivity: loss of MLH1, MSH2, MSH6, or PMS2 leading to high tumor mutational burden and neoantigen generation.
- Prognostic paradox: dMMR confers favorable prognosis in early stage II colon cancer, but poor prognosis in metastatic disease when treated with conventional chemotherapy alone.

### Case Generation Hooks
- A 49-year-old woman with Lynch syndrome presenting with synchronous cecal adenocarcinoma and multiple liver metastases testing positive for MSI-H.
- A patient with dMMR metastatic colon cancer achieving a complete radiographic response on first-line pembrolizumab inquiring about elective discontinuation at 2 years.

**Reviewer Gap**: Prospective trials evaluating whether neoadjuvant immune checkpoint inhibition can achieve pathological complete response and avoid radical resection in locally advanced dMMR colon cancer.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 33264544**: *Pembrolizumab in Microsatellite-Instability-High Advanced Colorectal Cancer (KEYNOTE-177)* (2020) [trial] - N Engl J Med
- **PMID 35427471**: *Five-year follow-up from the KEYNOTE-177 study of pembrolizumab versus chemotherapy for metastatic colorectal cancer* (2022) [trial] - Lancet Oncol
- **PMID 29355075**: *Nivolumab plus Low-Dose Ipilimumab in First-Line Metastatic Colorectal Cancer With Mismatch Repair-Deficient/Microsatellite Instability-High Tumors (CheckMate 142)* (2018) [trial] - J Clin Oncol
- **PMID 34637336**: *First-Line Nivolumab Plus Low-Dose Ipilimumab for Microsatellite Instability-High/Mismatch Repair-Deficient Metastatic Colorectal Cancer: Long-Term Follow-Up From CheckMate 142* (2022) [trial] - J Clin Oncol
- **PMID 26028255**: *PD-1 Blockade in Tumors with Mismatch-Repair Deficiency* (2015) [trial] - N Engl J Med
- **PMID 36307056**: *Metastatic colorectal cancer: ESMO Clinical Practice Guideline for diagnosis, treatment and follow-up* (2023) [guideline] - Ann Oncol

## [1147] MS initial presentation

**Cluster**: Neurology | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
The initial presentation of multiple sclerosis (MS) typically manifests as a clinically isolated syndrome (CIS) affecting the optic nerves, brainstem, or spinal cord in young adults, definitively diagnosed using the revised 2017 McDonald criteria requiring objective evidence of dissemination in space (DIS) and time (DIT).

### Core Teaching Points
- Under the revised 2017 McDonald criteria, dissemination in space (DIS) requires >=1 T2 hyperintense lesion in >=2 of 4 characteristic CNS areas: periventricular, cortical/juxtacortical, infratentorial, and spinal cord. (PMID 29277518)
- Dissemination in time (DIT) can be proven on a single baseline MRI by the simultaneous presence of asymptomatic gadolinium-enhancing and non-enhancing lesions, OR by the presence of CSF-specific oligoclonal bands, which now substitutes for DIT in patients fulfilling DIS criteria. (PMID 29277518)
- In the landmark Optic Neuritis Treatment Trial (ONTT), high-dose intravenous methylprednisolone (1 g daily for 3 days) followed by oral prednisone accelerated visual recovery compared to placebo, whereas oral prednisone alone at standard doses paradoxically increased the rate of recurrent optic neuritis. (PMID 1734248)
- International guidelines recommend early initiation of high-efficacy or moderate-efficacy disease-modifying therapy (DMT) upon confirmation of relapsing MS to suppress subclinical inflammation, reduce relapse rates, and prevent long-term neurological disability. (PMID 29352526, PMID 30410033)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Choosing between early aggressive high-efficacy DMTs (monoclonal antibodies) versus an escalation approach from platform therapies in mild initial presentations.
- Long-term clinical and radiological progression in patients diagnosed with radiologically isolated syndrome (RIS) without clinical relapse.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Role of CSF oligoclonal bands in the 2017 McDonald criteria: fulfills dissemination in time in patients with a typical CIS meeting DIS.
- Four characteristic anatomical locations for DIS: periventricular, cortical/juxtacortical, infratentorial, and spinal cord.
- ONTT trial pitfall: oral prednisone monotherapy without initial IV pulse methylprednisolone is contraindicated due to increased optic neuritis relapse.

### Case Generation Hooks
- A 26-year-old woman presenting with subacute painful loss of color vision in the right eye, relative afferent pupillary defect, and optic disc swelling.
- A 30-year-old man presenting with acute Lhermitte sign and lower limb numbness whose brain and spine MRI demonstrates periventricular and cervical cord demyelinating lesions.

**Reviewer Gap**: Validation of blood-based neurofilament light chain (NfL) and glial fibrillary acidic protein (GFAP) as point-of-care monitors of subclinical disease activity.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 29277518**: *Diagnosis of multiple sclerosis: 2017 revisions of the McDonald criteria* (2018) [guideline] - Lancet Neurol
- **PMID 29352526**: *ECTRIMS/EAN guideline on the pharmacological treatment of people with multiple sclerosis* (2018) [guideline] - Eur J Neurol
- **PMID 1734248**: *A randomized, controlled trial of corticosteroids in the treatment of acute optic neuritis* (1992) [trial] - N Engl J Med
- **PMID 30410033**: *Multiple sclerosis* (2018) [review] - Nat Rev Dis Primers

## [1148] Multidrug-resistant TB: MDR, XDR definitions, bedaquiline, delamanid, novel regimens

**Cluster**: Infectious Diseases / Pulmonology | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Multidrug-resistant tuberculosis (MDR-TB, resistant to isoniazid and rifampicin) and extensively drug-resistant tuberculosis (XDR-TB) represent major global health challenges, transformed by 6-month, all-oral, bedaquiline- and pretomanid-based therapeutic regimens.

### Core Teaching Points
- In the randomized TB-PRACTECAL trial by Nyang'wa and colleagues, a 24-week, all-oral regimen of bedaquiline, pretomanid, linezolid, and moxifloxacin (BPaLM) achieved an 89% favorable outcome rate compared with 52% on standard care regimens, with significantly lower treatment discontinuations and serious adverse events. (PMID 36546626)
- The ZeNix randomized trial proved that the 6-month BPaL regimen (bedaquiline, pretomanid, and linezolid) retained high treatment efficacy (89-93% favorable outcome) while lowering linezolid dosage from 1200 mg daily to 600 mg daily significantly reduced peripheral neuropathy (from 38% to 24%) and myelosuppression. (PMID 36053506)
- WHO 2022 consolidated guidelines recommend the 6-month all-oral BPaLM regimen (or BPaL if fluoroquinolone resistance is documented) as the preferred treatment for patients with MDR/RR-TB, replacing historical 18- to 24-month injectable regimens. (PMID 36729792)
- Under updated WHO definitions, extensively drug-resistant TB (XDR-TB) is redefined as MDR/RR-TB that is also resistant to any fluoroquinolone AND at least one additional Group A drug (bedaquiline or linezolid). (PMID 33504587)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Safety and pharmacokinetic profiles of bedaquiline and pretomanid in pregnant women and pediatric populations under 14 years.
- Surveillance and molecular mechanisms of emergent bedaquiline resistance (Rv0678 mutations) during programmatic implementation.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Composition of the WHO-preferred 6-month all-oral regimen (BPaLM): bedaquiline, pretomanid, linezolid (600 mg daily), and moxifloxacin.
- Redefined definition of XDR-TB: MDR/RR-TB resistant to a fluoroquinolone plus bedaquiline or linezolid.
- Safety monitoring requirements for bedaquiline: baseline and regular ECG monitoring for QTcF interval prolongation.
- Adverse effect mitigation in the ZeNix trial: reducing linezolid dose from 1200 mg to 600 mg daily preserves efficacy while minimizing toxic neuropathy.

### Case Generation Hooks
- A 34-year-old man whose sputum GeneXpert MTB/RIF confirms rifampicin resistance and line probe assay demonstrates rpoB mutation with preserved fluoroquinolone sensitivity.
- A patient with MDR-TB receiving the BPaL regimen who develops bilateral distal lower extremity numbness, burning paresthesias, and anemia at week 8 of therapy.

**Reviewer Gap**: Universal access to phenotypic and rapid targeted next-generation sequencing for bedaquiline and pretomanid resistance in high-burden tuberculosis settings.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 36546626**: *A 24-Week, All-Oral Regimen for Rifampin-Resistant Tuberculosis (TB-PRACTECAL)* (2022) [trial] - N Engl J Med
- **PMID 36053506**: *Bedaquiline-Pretomanid-Linezolid Regimens for Drug-Resistant Tuberculosis (ZeNix)* (2022) [trial] - N Engl J Med
- **PMID 36729792**: *WHO Consolidated Guidelines on Tuberculosis: Module 4: Treatment - Drug-Resistant Tuberculosis Treatment, 2022 Update* (2022) [guideline] - World Health Organization
- **PMID 33504587**: *Updated WHO definitions of extensively drug-resistant tuberculosis* (2021) [guideline] - Eur Respir J

## [1149] Multiple endocrine neoplasia type 1 MEN1: parathyroid, pituitary, pancreatic tumours

**Cluster**: Endocrinology / Oncology / Medical Genetics | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Multiple endocrine neoplasia type 1 (MEN1, Wermer syndrome) is an autosomal dominant tumor predisposition syndrome caused by germline mutations in the MEN1 tumor suppressor gene encoding menin, classically manifesting with the '3 Ps': parathyroid adenomas/hyperplasia (>95%), enteropancreatic neuroendocrine tumors (30-70%), and anterior pituitary adenomas (30-40%).

### Core Teaching Points
- Endocrine Society clinical guidelines state that primary hyperparathyroidism is the earliest and most common feature of MEN1, occurring in over 95% of patients by age 50, characterized by multiglandular parathyroid hyperplasia requiring subtotal (3.5-gland) parathyroidectomy or total parathyroidectomy with autotransplantation. (PMID 22723327)
- Positional cloning established that MEN1 is caused by inactivating germline mutations in the MEN1 gene on chromosome 11q13, which encodes the 610-amino acid nuclear scaffold protein menin that interacts with transcription factors and histone methyltransferases. (PMID 9103196)
- In long-term prospective registry cohorts from the French GTE group, duodenopancreatic neuroendocrine tumors (especially gastrinomas causing Zollinger-Ellison syndrome and non-functioning pNETs) and thymic carcinoids represent the primary cause of disease-related mortality in MEN1. (PMID 20084535)
- Screening guidelines recommend annual biochemical surveillance (serum calcium, PTH, gastrin, chromogranin A, prolactin, IGF-1) starting in childhood (ages 5-10) and biennial pancreatic/pituitary imaging (contrast MRI or endoscopic ultrasound) for early neoplastic detection in mutation carriers. (PMID 22723327, PMID 17014725)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Surgical resection criteria (size threshold 1 cm vs 2 cm) for asymptomatic non-functioning pancreatic neuroendocrine tumors in MEN1.
- Long-term efficacy of peptide receptor radionuclide therapy (PRRT) with 177Lu-DOTATATE in metastatic MEN1 duodenopancreatic NETs.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- The classic '3 Ps' triad of MEN1: Parathyroid (multiglandular), Pancreas/duodenum (gastrinoma, insulinoma, non-functioning), and Pituitary (prolactinoma).
- Inheritance and genetics: autosomal dominant tumor suppressor gene on chromosome 11q13 encoding menin.
- Surgical parathyroid approach: subtotal parathyroidectomy (not single adenoma resection) due to asymmetric multiglandular involvement.
- Leading causes of mortality: malignant pancreatic NETs and thymic/bronchial neuroendocrine carcinoids.

### Case Generation Hooks
- A 28-year-old woman presenting with recurrent nephrolithiasis, hypercalcemia, elevated PTH, and galactorrhea with amenorrhea found to have a pituitary microprolactinoma.
- A 42-year-old man with refractory peptic ulcer disease and recurrent diarrhea whose fasting gastrin is 1200 pg/mL undergoing genetic testing for MEN1.

**Reviewer Gap**: Novel menin inhibitors or epigenetic modifiers evaluated specifically to prevent neoplastic transformation in MEN1 mutation carriers.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 22723327**: *Clinical practice guidelines for multiple endocrine neoplasia type 1 (MEN1)* (2012) [guideline] - J Clin Endocrinol Metab
- **PMID 9103196**: *Positional cloning of the gene for multiple endocrine neoplasia-type 1* (1997) [trial] - Science
- **PMID 20084535**: *MEN1-related death: dynamic changes in causes of death across decades in the French GTE registry* (2010) [trial] - World J Surg
- **PMID 17014725**: *Multiple endocrine neoplasia type 1* (2006) [review] - Orphanet J Rare Dis

## [1150] Multiple endocrine neoplasia type 2 MEN2A and MEN2B: RET mutations, prophylactic thyroidectomy

**Cluster**: Endocrinology / Oncology / Medical Genetics | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Multiple endocrine neoplasia type 2 (MEN2A and MEN2B) is caused by activating gain-of-function germline mutations in the RET proto-oncogene; clinical management relies on genotype-phenotype risk stratification to guide timely prophylactic total thyroidectomy and prevent fatal medullary thyroid carcinoma.

### Core Teaching Points
- According to the American Thyroid Association (ATA) guidelines, germline RET proto-oncogene mutations are categorized into risk tiers: Highest Risk (MEN2B, codon M918T) mandates prophylactic total thyroidectomy within the first year of life (first months if feasible). (PMID 25810047)
- High-risk mutations (classical MEN2A, codon C634) mandate prophylactic total thyroidectomy before age 5 years to prevent nodal metastases from medullary thyroid carcinoma (MTC). (PMID 25810047, PMID 8099202)
- Pheochromocytoma develops in ~50% of patients with MEN2A and MEN2B (typically bilateral, benign); ATA guidelines mandate screening for pheochromocytoma (plasma free metanephrines) before neck surgery, and any identified pheochromocytoma must be resected first to avert intraoperative hypertensive crisis. (PMID 25810047, PMID 30860580)
- MEN2B is characterized clinically by the distinct syndromic triad of aggressive infancy-onset medullary thyroid carcinoma, pheochromocytoma, mucosal neuromas (lips and tongue), intestinal ganglioneuromatosis (chronic constipation/megacolon), and a marfanoid habitus, lacking primary hyperparathyroidism. (PMID 7906866, PMID 25810047)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Timing of prophylactic thyroidectomy in moderate-risk non-cysteine RET mutations guided by annual serum calcitonin doubling times.
- Long-term selective RET inhibitor (selpercatinib, pralsetinib) therapy as neoadjuvant cytoreduction in unresectable advanced MTC in MEN2.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Critical clinical sequence: always screen for and resect pheochromocytoma BEFORE performing thyroidectomy to prevent intraoperative hypertensive crisis.
- Codon M918T mutation indicates MEN2B: highest risk tier requiring prophylactic thyroidectomy within the first year of life.
- Phenotypic differences: MEN2A features MTC (100%), pheochromocytoma (50%), and primary hyperparathyroidism (20-30%); MEN2B features MTC, pheochromocytoma, mucosal neuromas, and marfanoid habitus, with NO hyperparathyroidism.
- Genetics: activating gain-of-function missense mutations in the RET proto-oncogene on chromosome 10q11.

### Case Generation Hooks
- A 3-year-old boy carrying the RET codon C634R mutation whose parents are counseled regarding prophylactic total thyroidectomy.
- A 19-year-old tall, slender man with bumpy lips, mucosal neuromas on the tongue, severe chronic constipation, and a 3 cm thyroid nodule found to have RET M918T mutation.

**Reviewer Gap**: Long-term registry outcomes comparing bilateral cortical-sparing adrenalectomy versus total adrenalectomy in preventing lifelong Addisonian crises in MEN2 pheochromocytoma.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 25810047**: *Revised American Thyroid Association guidelines for the management of medullary thyroid carcinoma* (2015) [guideline] - Thyroid
- **PMID 8099202**: *Germ-line mutations of the RET proto-oncogene in multiple endocrine neoplasia type 2A* (1993) [trial] - Nature
- **PMID 7906866**: *A mutation in the RET proto-oncogene associated with multiple endocrine neoplasia type 2B and sporadic medullary thyroid carcinoma* (1994) [trial] - Nature
- **PMID 30860580**: *Prognostic significance of RET mutation profiling in medullary thyroid carcinoma and timing of prophylactic surgery* (2019) [trial] - J Clin Endocrinol Metab

## [1151] Multiple Myeloma

**Cluster**: Haematology / Oncology | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Multiple myeloma is a clonal plasma cell neoplasm producing monoclonal immunoglobulins and osteolytic bone destruction, diagnosed according to the revised International Myeloma Working Group (IMWG) criteria encompassing traditional CRAB end-organ features and validated SLiM biomarkers of malignancy.

### Core Teaching Points
- The 2014 IMWG consensus criteria updated the diagnostic definition of multiple myeloma by adding three definitive 'SLiM' biomarkers that predict >=80% risk of progression to CRAB end-organ damage within 2 years: clonal bone marrow plasma cells >=60%, involved-to-uninvolved serum free light chain (FLC) ratio >=100, and >1 focal bone lesion (>=5 mm) on whole-body MRI. (PMID 25439696)
- Risk stratification is determined by the Revised International Staging System (R-ISS), integrating serum albumin, beta-2 microglobulin, serum LDH, and high-risk interphase fluorescence in situ hybridization (FISH) abnormalities: del(17p), t(4;14), and t(14;16). (PMID 26240224)
- IMWG consensus guidelines mandate low-dose whole-body CT (or whole-body MRI / 18F-FDG PET-CT) as first-line skeletal imaging, replacing conventional plain skeletal surveys due to superior sensitivity for detecting osteolytic lesions and marrow infiltration. (PMID 27511158, PMID 33798493)
- All symptomatic multiple myeloma patients require immediate supportive bone-targeted therapy with zoledronic acid or denosumab (denosumab preferred in patients with creatinine clearance <30 mL/min) to reduce skeletal-related events and pathologic fractures. (PMID 33798493)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Prognostic significance and therapeutic intensification for isolated 1q21 gain versus 1q21 amplification.
- Optimal interval for surveillance whole-body imaging in asymptomatic patients in sustained complete molecular remission.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- The 3 SLiM criteria: marrow plasma cells >=60%, serum FLC ratio >=100, and >1 focal MRI lesion >=5 mm.
- High-risk cytogenetic abnormalities defined in R-ISS: del(17p), t(4;14), and t(14;16).
- Selection of antiresorptive therapy: denosumab is preferred over zoledronic acid in severe renal failure.

### Case Generation Hooks
- A 63-year-old man presenting with acute back pain, normocytic anemia, serum creatinine 210 micromol/L, and multiple punched-out skull lesions.
- An asymptomatic patient with 65% clonal plasma cells on bone marrow biopsy but normal renal function, calcium, and plain radiographs meeting IMWG criteria.

**Reviewer Gap**: Prospective trials evaluating whether upfront CAR-T cell therapy can replace autologous stem cell transplantation in high-risk newly diagnosed disease.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 25439696**: *International Myeloma Working Group updated criteria for the diagnosis of multiple myeloma* (2014) [guideline] - Lancet Oncol
- **PMID 26240224**: *Revised International Staging System for Multiple Myeloma: A Report From International Myeloma Working Group* (2015) [guideline] - J Clin Oncol
- **PMID 27511158**: *Multiple myeloma: IMWG consensus guidelines for risk stratification and clinical management* (2016) [guideline] - Lancet Oncol
- **PMID 33798493**: *Multiple myeloma: diagnosis, risk stratification, and modern management principles* (2021) [review] - Lancet

## [1152] Multiple myeloma: autologous stem cell transplantation

**Cluster**: Haematology / Bone Marrow Transplantation | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
High-dose melphalan conditioning followed by autologous stem cell transplantation (ASCT) remains the established standard of care for transplant-eligible newly diagnosed multiple myeloma, significantly prolonging progression-free survival and deepening minimal residual disease negativity.

### Core Teaching Points
- In the landmark randomized IFM 2009 trial, early autologous stem cell transplantation following VRd induction significantly prolonged progression-free survival compared with VRd chemotherapy alone (median PFS 50 vs 36 months; HR 0.65; p<0.001) and doubled the rate of complete response. (PMID 28379796)
- The DETERMINATION trial confirmed that early ASCT combined with continuous lenalidomide maintenance until disease progression extended median PFS to an unprecedented 67.5 months versus 46.2 months in the chemotherapy-only arm (HR 0.70; p<0.001). (PMID 35660817)
- In a meta-analysis of four randomized trials by McCarthy and colleagues, maintenance therapy with single-agent lenalidomide following ASCT significantly reduced the risk of disease progression or death by 52% and conferred a statistically significant 2.5-year improvement in overall survival. (PMID 28742454)
- Standard conditioning is high-dose intravenous melphalan (200 mg/m2), dose-reduced to 140 mg/m2 in patients with severe renal failure (eGFR <30 mL/min/1.73m2) or advanced frailty. (PMID 12736280, PMID 28379796)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Whether delayed ASCT at first relapse achieves equivalent overall survival compared to upfront ASCT when quadruplet induction achieves MRD negativity.
- Role of tandem (double) ASCT in patients with high-risk cytogenetics (e.g., del(17p) plus 1q amplification) receiving modern maintenance.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Results of IFM 2009 and DETERMINATION: significant PFS superiority of early ASCT over chemotherapy alone.
- Standard conditioning regimen: high-dose melphalan 200 mg/m2.
- Survival benefit of post-transplant lenalidomide maintenance: significant prolongation of both PFS and overall survival.

### Case Generation Hooks
- A 56-year-old previously fit executive completing 4 cycles of Dara-VRd induction achieving partial response evaluated for autologous stem cell harvest.
- A 64-year-old patient 100 days post-ASCT in complete remission initiating continuous daily lenalidomide maintenance therapy.

**Reviewer Gap**: Randomized trials determining whether post-transplant maintenance can be safely stopped in patients with sustained 3-year bone marrow MRD negativity.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 28379796**: *Lenalidomide, Bortezomib, and Dexamethasone with Transplantation for Myeloma (IFM 2009)* (2017) [trial] - N Engl J Med
- **PMID 35660817**: *Triplet Therapy, Transplantation, and Maintenance until Progression in Myeloma (DETERMINATION)* (2022) [trial] - N Engl J Med
- **PMID 28742454**: *Lenalidomide Maintenance After Autologous Stem-Cell Transplantation in Newly Diagnosed Multiple Myeloma: A Meta-Analysis* (2017) [meta-analysis] - J Clin Oncol
- **PMID 12736280**: *High-dose chemotherapy with hematopoietic stem-cell rescue for multiple myeloma (MRC Myeloma VII)* (2003) [trial] - N Engl J Med

## [1153] Multiple myeloma: daratumumab-based frontline therapy

**Cluster**: Haematology / Oncology | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Incorporation of daratumumab (anti-CD38 monoclonal antibody) into frontline quadruplet induction regimens has redefined the standard of care for transplant-eligible newly diagnosed multiple myeloma, achieving high minimal residual disease negativity rates and durable disease suppression.

### Core Teaching Points
- In the phase 3 CASSIOPEIA trial, adding daratumumab to bortezomib, thalidomide, and dexamethasone (Dara-VTd) pre- and post-ASCT significantly increased stringent complete response rates (29% vs 20%; p=0.001) and reduced the risk of progression or death by 53% (HR 0.47; p<0.0001). (PMID 31171419)
- The randomized phase 2 GRIFFIN trial demonstrated that daratumumab plus VRd (Dara-VRd) followed by ASCT and Dara-R maintenance achieved high confirmed MRD negativity (<10^-5) of 64% versus 30% for VRd alone, with 4-year PFS of 87.2% vs 70.0%. (PMID 32321181, PMID 36870377)
- In the phase 3 PERSEUS trial, subcutaneous daratumumab combined with VRd induction and consolidation followed by daratumumab-lenalidomide maintenance significantly prolonged progression-free survival (48-month PFS 84.3% vs 67.7%; HR 0.42; p<0.001) and achieved 75.2% MRD negativity. (PMID 38084222)
- In transplant-ineligible newly diagnosed multiple myeloma, the landmark MAIA trial established that daratumumab plus lenalidomide and dexamethasone (Dara-Rd) significantly prolonged overall survival compared to Rd alone (5-year OS 66.3% vs 53.1%; HR 0.68; p=0.001). (PMID 31141632)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal duration of dual daratumumab plus lenalidomide maintenance post-transplant in patients with confirmed sustained MRD negativity at 24 months.
- Infection prophylaxis protocols (PJP, shingles, encapsulated bacteria) during multi-year continuous CD38-directed monoclonal antibody therapy.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Current preferred frontline quadruplet for transplant-eligible myeloma: Dara-VRd based on GRIFFIN and PERSEUS trials.
- Standard frontline regimen for transplant-ineligible myeloma: Dara-Rd based on the MAIA trial.
- Daratumumab interference with pre-transfusion blood compatibility testing: binds CD38 on reagent RBCs causing false-positive indirect antiglobulin tests (mitigated by dithiothreitol DTT treatment).

### Case Generation Hooks
- A 58-year-old woman presenting with new symptomatic multiple myeloma starting Dara-VRd induction requiring baseline extended RBC phenotype matching.
- A 76-year-old man with newly diagnosed IgG myeloma deemed ineligible for high-dose melphalan initiating frontline continuous Dara-Rd.

**Reviewer Gap**: Real-world studies evaluating whether subcutaneous daratumumab delivery overcomes healthcare access disparities associated with prolonged intravenous infusions.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 31171419**: *Bortezomib, thalidomide, and dexamethasone with or without daratumumab before and after autologous stem-cell transplantation for newly diagnosed multiple myeloma (CASSIOPEIA)* (2019) [trial] - Lancet
- **PMID 32321181**: *Daratumumab plus bortezomib, lenalidomide, and dexamethasone in newly diagnosed multiple myeloma: primary results of the randomized GRIFFIN study* (2020) [trial] - Blood
- **PMID 36870377**: *Daratumumab plus bortezomib, lenalidomide, and dexamethasone in newly diagnosed multiple myeloma: 4-year follow-up of GRIFFIN* (2023) [trial] - Lancet Haematol
- **PMID 38084222**: *Daratumumab, Bortezomib, Lenalidomide, and Dexamethasone for Multiple Myeloma (PERSEUS)* (2024) [trial] - N Engl J Med
- **PMID 31141632**: *Daratumumab plus Lenalidomide and Dexamethasone for Untreated Myeloma (MAIA)* (2019) [trial] - N Engl J Med

## [1154] Multiple myeloma daratumumab-based regimens

**Cluster**: Haematology / Clinical Oncology | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Daratumumab-based combination regimens span the entirety of the multiple myeloma therapeutic continuum, providing unprecedented response depth and overall survival prolongation in both newly diagnosed transplant-ineligible disease and relapsed/refractory settings.

### Core Teaching Points
- In transplant-ineligible newly diagnosed multiple myeloma, long-term follow-up of the phase 3 MAIA trial proved that daratumumab plus lenalidomide and dexamethasone (Dara-Rd) extended median progression-free survival beyond 5 years (61.9 vs 34.4 months; HR 0.53; p<0.0001) and significantly improved 5-year overall survival (66.3% vs 53.1%). (PMID 34655533, PMID 31141632)
- In the phase 3 ALCYONE trial, adding daratumumab to bortezomib, melphalan, and prednisone (Dara-VMP) in transplant-ineligible patients reduced the risk of death by 40% (HR 0.60; p=0.0003) and significantly increased minimal residual disease negativity (28% vs 7%). (PMID 29231133, PMID 31836199)
- In relapsed or refractory multiple myeloma, the phase 3 POLLUX trial proved that Dara-Rd reduced the risk of disease progression or death by 63% (HR 0.37; p<0.001) compared with Rd alone, achieving unprecedented depth of response. (PMID 27705267)
- In the CASTOR trial for relapsed myeloma, daratumumab combined with bortezomib and dexamethasone (Dara-Vd) significantly extended progression-free survival (HR 0.39; p<0.001) compared with bortezomib-dexamethasone doublet therapy. (PMID 27557301)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Sequencing and efficacy of anti-BCMA CAR-T cell or bispecific T-cell engager therapies following progression on anti-CD38 monoclonal antibody therapy.
- Subcutaneous vs intravenous daratumumab formulation differences regarding long-term local site reactions and systemic infusion reactions.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- PFS milestone in the MAIA trial: median PFS exceeded 5 years (61.9 months) with Dara-Rd in elderly transplant-ineligible patients.
- Trial pairing: POLLUX evaluated Dara-Rd whereas CASTOR evaluated Dara-Vd in relapsed/refractory disease.
- Laboratory interference: daratumumab appears as a small IgG kappa band on serum protein electrophoresis and immunofixation, mimicking residual disease.

### Case Generation Hooks
- A 78-year-old newly diagnosed myeloma patient starting Dara-Rd achieving partial response after 3 cycles with excellent functional tolerance.
- A 67-year-old myeloma patient experiencing first biochemical relapse while on lenalidomide maintenance evaluated for daratumumab-based salvage.

**Reviewer Gap**: Cost-effectiveness models comparing continuous frontline daratumumab versus fixed-duration therapy in publicly funded healthcare delivery systems.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 34655533**: *Overall survival results with daratumumab, lenalidomide, and dexamethasone versus lenalidomide and dexamethasone in transplant-ineligible newly diagnosed multiple myeloma (MAIA)* (2021) [trial] - Lancet Oncol
- **PMID 31141632**: *Daratumumab plus Lenalidomide and Dexamethasone for Untreated Myeloma* (2019) [trial] - N Engl J Med
- **PMID 29231133**: *Daratumumab plus Bortezomib, Melphalan, and Prednisone for Untreated Myeloma (ALCYONE)* (2018) [trial] - N Engl J Med
- **PMID 31836199**: *Overall survival with daratumumab, bortezomib, melphalan, and prednisone in newly diagnosed multiple myeloma (ALCYONE)* (2020) [trial] - Lancet
- **PMID 27705267**: *Daratumumab, Lenalidomide, and Dexamethasone for Multiple Myeloma (POLLUX)* (2016) [trial] - N Engl J Med
- **PMID 27557301**: *Daratumumab, Bortezomib, and Dexamethasone for Multiple Myeloma (CASTOR)* (2016) [trial] - N Engl J Med

## [1155] Multiple Myeloma Induction and Transplant

**Cluster**: Haematology / Cellular Therapy | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Induction therapy in transplant-eligible multiple myeloma aims to achieve deep initial cytoreduction and maximal minimal residual disease negativity prior to high-dose melphalan consolidation, with contemporary quadruplet regimens outperforming historical triplets.

### Core Teaching Points
- In the phase 3 GMMG-HD7 trial by Goldschmidt and colleagues, adding the anti-CD38 antibody isatuximab to VRd induction (Isa-VRd) significantly increased the rate of MRD negativity at the end of induction (50.1% vs 35.6%; OR 1.83; p<0.001) prior to autologous transplant. (PMID 35688172)
- In the randomized STaMINA trial, a single autologous stem cell transplant followed by lenalidomide maintenance demonstrated equivalent overall survival compared with tandem ASCT or post-transplant VRd consolidation, establishing single ASCT as the standard for standard-risk disease. (PMID 30645474)
- The randomized EMN02/HO95 trial confirmed that upfront ASCT significantly improved 5-year progression-free survival (53.5% vs 41.9%; HR 0.74; p=0.0001) and overall survival compared to intensified conventional chemotherapy (bortezomib, melphalan, and prednisone). (PMID 32470438)
- Induction regimens should avoid alkylating agents (e.g., oral melphalan) to prevent stem cell toxicity and preserve hematopoietic bone marrow stem cell collection reserves. (PMID 28379796, PMID 32470438)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Role of tandem ASCT specifically in patients with double-hit or triple-hit cytogenetics receiving quadruplet induction.
- Optimum number of induction cycles (4 cycles vs 6 cycles) prior to peripheral blood CD34+ stem cell mobilization.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Rationale for avoiding alkylators (melphalan) during induction: avoids stem cell damage and marrow failure during apheresis.
- Primary finding of the STaMINA trial: single ASCT with lenalidomide maintenance is non-inferior to tandem ASCT in unselected standard-risk patients.
- GMMG-HD7 trial outcome: addition of isatuximab to VRd significantly increased pre-transplant MRD negativity.
- Standard mobilization regimen: G-CSF with or without plerixafor (CXCR4 antagonist).

### Case Generation Hooks
- A 52-year-old man completing 4 cycles of Isa-VRd undergoing CD34+ peripheral stem cell apheresis collecting 6.5 x 10^6 CD34+ cells/kg.
- A high-risk myeloma patient with del(17p) and t(4;14) discussing the evidence for single versus tandem transplantation.

**Reviewer Gap**: Biomarker-guided prospective trials examining whether patients achieving MRD negativity (<10^-6) after quadruplet induction can safely defer upfront ASCT.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 35688172**: *Addition of isatuximab to bortezomib, lenalidomide, and dexamethasone as induction therapy for newly diagnosed, transplant-eligible multiple myeloma (GMMG-HD7)* (2022) [trial] - Lancet Oncol
- **PMID 30645474**: *Comparison of Autologous Hematopoietic Cell Transplant (Single vs Tandem) and Consolidation in Newly Diagnosed Multiple Myeloma (STaMINA)* (2019) [trial] - J Clin Oncol
- **PMID 32470438**: *Autologous stem cell transplantation versus bortezomib-melphalan-prednisone for newly diagnosed multiple myeloma (EMN02/HO95)* (2020) [trial] - Lancet Haematol
- **PMID 28379796**: *Lenalidomide, Bortezomib, and Dexamethasone with Transplantation for Myeloma (IFM 2009)* (2017) [trial] - N Engl J Med

## [1156] Multiple myeloma: VRd versus Rd for newly diagnosed disease

**Cluster**: Haematology / Oncology | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
The triplet regimen of bortezomib, lenalidomide, and dexamethasone (VRd) established the superiority of proteasome inhibitor plus immunomodulatory drug (IMiD) combination therapy over doublet regimens for newly diagnosed multiple myeloma, delivering significant progression-free and overall survival advantages.

### Core Teaching Points
- In the landmark phase 3 SWOG S0777 trial by Durie and colleagues, newly diagnosed multiple myeloma patients randomized to VRd experienced significantly longer progression-free survival (median 43 vs 30 months; HR 0.71; p=0.0018) and overall survival (median 75 vs 64 months; HR 0.71; p=0.025) compared to lenalidomide and dexamethasone (Rd) alone. (PMID 28017648)
- Long-term 7-year follow-up of SWOG S0777 confirmed that the survival benefit of adding bortezomib was sustained across both younger (<65 years) and older (>=65 years) cohorts, establishing triplet therapy as the standard baseline comparator. (PMID 32393732)
- The phase 3 FIRST trial demonstrated that continuous Rd until progression prolonged PFS compared with melphalan-prednisone-thalidomide (MPT), establishing continuous immunomodulation as a foundational backbone. (PMID 25184863)
- NCCN and international clinical practice guidelines designate VRd (or quadruplet Dara-VRd) as a preferred category 1 standard of care for initial therapy of multiple myeloma. (PMID 36622830)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Use of dose-attenuated 'VRd-lite' (weekly subcutaneous bortezomib, 15 mg lenalidomide) in frail older adults versus doublet Rd.
- Thromboprophylaxis selection (aspirin vs therapeutic anticoagulation with DOACs or LMWH) in patients with multiple cardiovascular risk factors receiving VRd.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Key outcome of the SWOG S0777 trial: addition of bortezomib to Rd conferred both a 13-month PFS advantage and an 11-month overall survival advantage.
- Mandatory prophylaxis during VRd therapy: acyclovir/valacyclovir for herpes zoster reactivation (due to bortezomib) and antithrombotic prophylaxis (due to lenalidomide).
- Neurotoxicity reduction: subcutaneous administration and weekly dosing of bortezomib dramatically reduces peripheral neuropathy compared to IV twice-weekly dosing.

### Case Generation Hooks
- A 67-year-old newly diagnosed myeloma patient initiating VRd who requires counseling on shingles prevention and deep venous thrombosis prophylaxis.
- A patient developing grade 2 sensory peripheral neuropathy after cycle 3 of VRd requiring bortezomib dose attenuation.

**Reviewer Gap**: Randomized phase 3 trials comparing frontline VRd-lite against doublet Rd in strictly defined frail geriatric populations.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 28017648**: *Bortezomib with lenalidomide and dexamethasone versus lenalidomide and dexamethasone alone in patients with newly diagnosed myeloma without intent for immediate autologous stem-cell transplant (SWOG S0777)* (2017) [trial] - Lancet
- **PMID 32393732**: *Longer term follow-up of the SWOG S0777 trial confirming sustained overall survival superiority of VRd over Rd* (2020) [trial] - Blood Cancer J
- **PMID 25184863**: *Lenalidomide and dexamethasone in newly diagnosed multiple myeloma (FIRST)* (2014) [trial] - N Engl J Med
- **PMID 36622830**: *Multiple Myeloma, Version 2.2023, NCCN Clinical Practice Guidelines in Oncology* (2023) [guideline] - J Natl Compr Canc Netw

## [1157] multiple sclerosis

**Cluster**: Neurology | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Multiple sclerosis (MS) is an immune-mediated chronic inflammatory and neurodegenerative disease of the central nervous system characterized by multifocal demyelination, astrocytic gliosis, and axonal transection, manifesting as relapsing-remitting or progressive disease requiring early diagnostic McDonald criteria verification and disease-modifying treatment.

### Core Teaching Points
- According to the revised 2017 McDonald criteria, diagnosis requires objective clinical and neuroimaging demonstration of lesions disseminated in space (DIS, involving >=2 of 4 typical CNS regions) and time (DIT, simultaneous contrast-enhancing and non-enhancing lesions or new lesions on follow-up). (PMID 29277518)
- The 2017 McDonald criteria established that the presence of CSF-specific oligoclonal bands (unique bands present in CSF but absent in matched serum) substitutes for the requirement of dissemination in time in patients with typical clinically isolated syndrome (CIS) fulfilling DIS criteria. (PMID 29277518)
- Clinical disease phenotype is standardized by the Lublin classification into clinically isolated syndrome (CIS), relapsing-remitting MS (RRMS, ~85% of presentations), secondary progressive MS (SPMS), and primary progressive MS (PPMS, ~15%), each characterized by disease activity (clinical relapses or new MRI lesions) and progression. (PMID 24871874)
- ECTRIMS/EAN guidelines recommend starting disease-modifying therapy early in patients with CIS and abnormal baseline brain MRI showing >=2 demyelinating lesions, as early treatment significantly reduces conversion to clinically definite MS and delays long-term disability accumulation. (PMID 29352526, PMID 30410033)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Stopping disease-modifying therapies in stable older patients (>60 years) with non-active secondary progressive MS.
- Long-term neuroprotective impact of Bruton tyrosine kinase (BTK) inhibitors on smoldering non-active progression in progressive MS.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- CSF oligoclonal bands role in 2017 McDonald criteria: allows formal diagnosis of MS at initial presentation if DIS is met.
- The 4 anatomical zones for Dissemination in Space: periventricular, cortical/juxtacortical, infratentorial, and spinal cord.
- Definition of active secondary progressive MS: gradual disability worsening over >=6-12 months independent of relapses, but with superimposed relapses or new gadolinium-enhancing lesions.

### Case Generation Hooks
- A 27-year-old woman presenting with unilateral optic neuritis whose brain MRI shows 4 periventricular Dawson fingers and 1 enhancing spinal cord lesion.
- A 42-year-old man with a 10-year history of RRMS who reports progressive insidious difficulty walking over the past 18 months without discrete acute relapses.

**Reviewer Gap**: Validation of ultra-high field (7-Tesla) MRI detection of central vein sign and paramagnetic rim lesions as definitive imaging criteria.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 29277518**: *Diagnosis of multiple sclerosis: 2017 revisions of the McDonald criteria* (2018) [guideline] - Lancet Neurol
- **PMID 24871874**: *Defining the clinical course of multiple sclerosis: the 2013 revisions* (2014) [guideline] - Neurology
- **PMID 29352526**: *ECTRIMS/EAN guideline on the pharmacological treatment of people with multiple sclerosis* (2018) [guideline] - Eur J Neurol
- **PMID 30410033**: *Multiple sclerosis* (2018) [review] - Nat Rev Dis Primers

## [1158] Multiple sclerosis disease-modifying therapy

**Cluster**: Neurology / Neuropharmacology | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Disease-modifying therapies (DMTs) for multiple sclerosis have expanded into highly effective targeted immunomodulatory agents, including B-cell-depleting monoclonal antibodies, selective cell adhesion blockers, and oral pulsed immune reconstitution therapies that markedly reduce relapse rates and delay disability progression.

### Core Teaching Points
- In the twin OPERA I and OPERA II phase 3 trials, the humanized anti-CD20 monoclonal antibody ocrelizumab significantly reduced annualized relapse rates by 46-47% (p<0.001) and reduced 12-week confirmed disability progression by 40% compared with subcutaneous interferon beta-1a in relapsing MS. (PMID 28002311)
- In the phase 3 ORATORIO trial, ocrelizumab became the first disease-modifying therapy to demonstrate efficacy in primary progressive MS, significantly reducing 12-week confirmed disability progression by 24% (p=0.03) and decreasing total brain volume loss compared to placebo. (PMID 28002318)
- In the AFFIRM trial, the alpha-4 integrin antagonist natalizumab reduced annualized relapse rates by 68% (p<0.001) and reduced sustained disability progression by 42%, though long-term therapy mandates strict anti-JCV antibody index surveillance to stratify progressive multifocal leukoencephalopathy (PML) risk. (PMID 16510744)
- ECTRIMS/EAN consensus guidelines support early use of high-efficacy DMTs (ocrelizumab, ofatumumab, natalizumab, alemtuzumab) in patients with highly active disease (frequent disabling relapses or multiple spinal/infratentorial gadolinium-enhancing lesions). (PMID 29352526)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Comparative long-term benefits of early intensive therapy versus rapid escalation in patients presenting with moderate baseline lesion loads.
- Safety and rebound disease flare following discontinuation of natalizumab or S1P receptor modulators (fingolimod) prior to pregnancy.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- First DMT approved with proven disability reduction in primary progressive MS: ocrelizumab (ORATORIO trial).
- Major safety concern with natalizumab: progressive multifocal leukoencephalopathy (PML) caused by JC virus reactivation.
- Pre-treatment screening required prior to anti-CD20 therapy (ocrelizumab, ofatumumab): hepatitis B serologies (HBsAg, anti-HBc) and baseline quantitative immunoglobulins (IgG, IgM).
- First-dose monitoring with S1P modulators (fingolimod): 6-hour observation for transient bradycardia and AV block.

### Case Generation Hooks
- A 29-year-old woman with highly active RRMS (3 relapses in 12 months, 5 gadolinium-enhancing lesions) initiating high-efficacy therapy with anti-CD20 monoclonal antibody.
- A patient on natalizumab for 3 years whose anti-JCV antibody index rises from negative to 2.8 evaluating a therapeutic switch to prevent PML.

**Reviewer Gap**: Long-term registry studies assessing hypogammaglobulinemia and infection risk during continuous decade-long anti-CD20 B-cell depletion.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 28002311**: *Ocrelizumab versus Interferon Beta-1a in Relapsing Multiple Sclerosis (OPERA I and OPERA II)* (2017) [trial] - N Engl J Med
- **PMID 28002318**: *Ocrelizumab versus Placebo in Primary Progressive Multiple Sclerosis (ORATORIO)* (2017) [trial] - N Engl J Med
- **PMID 16510744**: *A randomized, placebo-controlled trial of natalizumab for relapsing multiple sclerosis (AFFIRM)* (2006) [trial] - N Engl J Med
- **PMID 29352526**: *ECTRIMS/EAN guideline on the pharmacological treatment of people with multiple sclerosis* (2018) [guideline] - Eur J Neurol
